- [x] T306: the score vector - named dimensions, visible components, honest gaps
  - Date: 2026-09-14
  - Session: CC-20260812-k4m9
  - What changed: `services/growthJourney/scoring/dimensions.ts` declares sec 5.3's ten dimensions and sec 5.4's nine as 16 registry entries (the three names both sections use verbatim - problem clarity, urgency, qualification completeness - are ONE entry carrying both programmes; where the two sections use different words for adjacent ideas they stay separate under the spec's own names, because merging them would invent a vocabulary neither uses). `scoreVector.ts` is a pure `scoreSubject(signals, program)` with five scorers and no I/O. The point of the task is the SEVEN of ten and SIX of nine that have no source in this codebase: each carries `source: 'none'`, scores `null`, appears in `gaps`, and - this is the part that matters - carries a REASON naming the nearest candidate and what it actually measures, because "no source" on its own invites the next person to invent one. Relationship engagement fails because every candidate measures our own outbound; authority fails because the only signal is a title regex scoring an Apollo person before import; problem clarity fails because `idea_input` is unparsed free text and parsing it would be an AI judgement, which may never be authoritative for a score; friction/risk fails because the nearest candidate is a bounce count, which is a channel problem and not a deal risk. Three dimensions per programme DO have sources and use real populated columns: fit from firmographics, intent from observed visitor signals plus declared systems, urgency from `evaluating_90_days`, and for consulting solution fit from `selected_systems`/`technology_stack` and technical feasibility from `maturity_score`. `lead_temperature`, `opportunity_scores` and `recommended_offer` are recorded as ZERO-POINT factors on a dimension that can never score - four writers with disjoint vocabularies, an in-place overwrite with no history, and a competing offer vocabulary are not a measurement.
  - Verification: 122 suites / 2,093 tests green across db, models, explorerGrowth, growthJourney, routing and the content-sync script; 29 tests of its own; scoped `tsc` exit 0 with a planted error in each of the three new files, every one firing and naming its own file; seven mutations all caught and reverted by string replace with md5 restored - including both the plan's own (a sourceless dimension defaulting to 50; one dimension removed) and five more for the properties that make the task honest (a zero default in the scorer, a summary computed over partial values, a measured zero turned back into null, `lead_temperature` given points, and a sourceless dimension quietly acquiring a source name, which the pinned 7-of-10 count catches first).
  - Notes (a measured zero and an unmeasured null are different answers): this is the distinction the whole file turns on, and it is tested four ways. Signals that were READ and were empty score 0 - we looked, and there was nothing, which is a measurement. Signals that were never read score `null` with a named gap. An unanswered `evaluating_90_days` is `null`; an answered "no" scores 10, because that is an answer. A dimension defaulted to 0 reads as "measured, and low" and a 50 as "measured, and average", and both are claims about a subject nobody measured. The summary follows the same rule: it is the weighted mean over the dimensions that HAVE a source and is null if any one of them came back null, worked by hand in one test (fit 20 x .4 + intent 0 x .3 + urgency 100 x .3 = 38), and the sourceless seven deliberately do NOT suppress it - if they did it could never exist and the field would be decorative.
  - Notes (the zero-default scan caught my own comment, for the second time this session): the raw-text scan the plan asked for fired on the doc comment that DESCRIBES the forbidden shape. Code defaults nothing; prose naming the shape is the opposite of committing it - the same finding T304's cutoff rule produced. The scan now strips comments and carries a control proving both directions. One of my fixtures was also wrong: "signals READ and empty score 0" expected 0 and got 10, because that lead also declared `selected_systems`, which adds an intent factor - declared intent outranks observed under sec 7.1, so 10 was correct and the FIXTURE was what needed fixing. The declaration bump now has its own case.

A learner programme returns `available: false` with a gap naming that Explorer owns learner scores: a learner dimension set here would be a second learner scorer.
  - Notes (ATTEMPT 1 FAILED at 7/12, and the findings were right): three different ways an unmeasured input became a number, in the one task whose whole purpose was to refuse that. `numeric()` stripped non-digits and then trusted `Number('')`, which is 0, so `annual_revenue: 'confidential'` scored 10 points for "Annual revenue recorded"; `list()` returned `[]` for an empty JSONB array, which is truthy, so an empty list earned the same points as a populated one and labelled itself "0 technologies named"; and together a lead of all empty strings and empty arrays scored `summary 26`. Worst of the three: `leads.evaluating_90_days` is `allowNull: false, defaultValue: false`, so my null branch could never fire from the live column and EVERY lead nobody asked scored urgency 10 labelled "Not evaluating within 90 days" - a column default rendered as a declared measurement, on the one dimension both programmes depend on. Now only a positive answer is a measurement, `false` and `null` share a gap reason naming the default, and a negative counts only when a caller confirms the question was put (a seam, because nothing in the repo records the asking). The consequence is stated rather than hidden: almost every subject now has no urgency and therefore no summary, which is the true state of the data.

The source is renamed `advisory_ai_maturity`, the factor says where the number comes from, and the sentence is corrected in place; it stays a source because AI may rank and recommend and this touches no date, price, seat, consent or brand decision - what it may not be is invisible. - [x] T307: the Colaberry Business lifecycle, its overlays, and the profile projection
  - Date: 2026-09-14

  - What changed: `lifecycle/businessLifecycle.ts` is sec 5.3's eight states as a PURE `classifyBusinessState`, built on Explorer's own machinery rather than a second state machine - read the evidence for NOW, apply monotonicity against the previous state, then move `state_entered_at` only on a real change, in that order. The split that matters: the DISCOVERY ladder (new -> problem identified -> exploring -> qualified) is knowledge and never steps down, because knowing someone has a problem does not stop being true when they go quiet - they gain a STALLED overlay instead; the COMMERCIAL states (discovery ready -> scoping -> proposal) may regress, because a cancelled meeting means they are no longer discovery-ready and pretending otherwise keeps proposal-stage messages going to someone who walked away; and CUSTOMER is terminal. A regressing deal floors at QUALIFIED_OPPORTUNITY rather than falling to NEW, since the deal cooled but the knowledge did not evaporate. Evidence comes only from sources that exist and are populated: `idea_input`/classification intent, `selected_systems`/classification path, counted `interaction_outcomes` (replied, booked_meeting, answered - the BUYER acting, which is what separates a qualified opportunity from one we merely like), `appointments`, `pipeline_stage` as a signal, and an enrolment for CUSTOMER. `profileService.upsertProfile` writes the projection and, on a real change, exactly one `growth_journey_transitions` row of the new `state_changed` type through Phase 2's `recordTransition` - not a second writer and not a second idempotency scheme.
  - Verification: 125 suites / 2,164 tests green across db, models, explorerGrowth, growthJourney, routing and the content-sync script; 37 lifecycle tests and 18 projection tests at `c5951aea`, 48 and 24 after the retry; scoped `tsc` exit 0 over six files with a planted error in each of the four new ones, every one firing and naming its own file; seven mutations all caught and reverted by string replace with md5 restored - including both the plan's own (a downward transition allowed; a state renamed) and five more (the timestamp reset every run, the declared gap made reachable, CUSTOMER made non-terminal, overlays accumulating, and a transition written when nothing changed).
  - Notes (one state has no source, and it is DECLARED): `SOLUTION_SCOPING` cannot be entered today - nothing records a scoping conversation, a statement of work or a scope document against a lead, `activities` has no type vocabulary for it, `pipeline_stage` jumps straight from meeting_scheduled to proposal_sent, and there is no opportunities table at all. Explorer's `DEFERRED_RULES` precedent: the absence sits in `DEFERRED_STATES` with its reason and its target phase, and a test drives six different input shapes through the machine asserting none produces it. A state nobody can reach is much better declared than silently unreachable. Same discipline for overlays: sec 5.3 names NONE (it lists states and score dimensions only), so the six here do not pretend to be the spec's vocabulary and each names the source it derives from, while three more that would be useful - SPONSOR_IDENTIFIED, BUDGET_CONFIRMED, SECURITY_REVIEW - are declared absent with their reasons. STALLED is the one duration-based overlay and it works here where Explorer defers all of its, because `state_entered_at` is a real column while Explorer's overlays have no per-overlay timestamp.
  - Notes (a raw-text scan hit my own prose for the FOURTH time this session): the scan proving this lifecycle never writes `pipeline_stage` also bans `advancePipelineStage`, and my header names that function while explaining why the column is read-only here - so it failed on the explanation. T304's cutoff rule and T306's zero-default rule did exactly the same thing. The resolution is always the same and is now applied consistently: the scan reads CODE with comments stripped, prose may name what the code must not do, and a control proves both halves. Worth stating as a pattern rather than fixing quietly a fourth time - when a test reads raw source, the comments in that file are part of its input.
  - Assumptions: `interaction_outcomes` is used as evidence for a STATE here while T306 declares `relationship_engagement` sourceless for the SCORE - not a contradiction: T306's pin is about its registry's count and its `deferred_source` field records that the table is real, and a lifecycle state and a score dimension are different questions about the same row. The profile is written BEFORE the transition, because the reverse order would leave a transition claiming a state the profile does not hold. **A failed audit write therefore LOSES that row - it is not deferred**, which the retry below corrects: an earlier version of this bullet said "the next run records the change", and it cannot, because the change is detected from the projection that has already moved. And `state_entered_at` is passed through from the lifecycle rather than recomputed in the service, because two places deciding when that clock resets is two places to get it wrong.

Worse than the gap: the `it.each` rows NAMED the pairs they did not test ("holds at EXPLORING_SOLUTIONS rather than falling to PROBLEM_IDENTIFIED") while the callback ignored the second tuple element - a label claiming a property the assertion never checked, which is the most expensive kind of test to have because it reads as coverage. All six downward pairs are now driven by inputs that really produce the lower state, and each row first asserts that its input does produce it. The header now says LOST, names the consequence and the Phase 4 repair, and two tests pin it.

---

## What this ships

Read-only. Twelve GETs behind an admin guard, no write path, no send, no flag touched.
§27's seven write routes are Phase B, where a human sees the consequence as they flip it.

| Surface | What it answers |
|---|---|
| Overview | 153 decisions on the latest run: 11 waited, 142 actionable, 130 with content, 12 gaps |
| Journey | The roster by state and overlay, with scores, filtered and paginated |
| Decisions | Today's decisions, filterable, each row linking to its Why |
| Why | The winner **and every loser with its reason** — 142 of 153 have suppressed candidates |
| Shadow | What would have gone out: 130 emails + 12 lesson recommendations, 0 executed |
| Content | 646 assets / 552 active, coverage per purpose, and the gaps with their reasons |

## The three defects the verification loop caught before deploy

1. **A binding bug that would have 500'd the Content tab** — every test passed, because they
   all asserted on the SQL template and none on the bound value.
2. **A missing `try`/`catch` that would have restarted the backend container** on any query
   rejection across twelve routes.
3. **242 lines of service with no test at all.**

All three were mine, all three passed my own checks first, and none would have been visible
from a green suite.

## Growth Journey OS - Phase 5, 2026-09-19

- [x] T501: one open handoff per PERSON per brand (7A), and every trigger that lands on an open row recorded on it (8A)
  - Date: 2026-09-19
  - Session: CC-20260812-k4m9
  - What changed: a new sibling statements module, `db/growthJourneyPhase5Statements.ts`, opens Phase 5's schema the way `growthJourneyPhase4Statements.ts` opened Phase 4's - `ensureGrowthJourneySchema.ts` is AT the 500-line ceiling, so the import it needed was paid for by folding the two-line Phase 4 comment into one, and both lists are spread on one line before the brands ALTER the statements test pins last (the file is 499 lines, asserted). Its first statement is **7A**: `growth_journey_handoffs_open_lead_unique` on `(lead_id, brand_id) WHERE status IN ('queued', 'assigned', 'accepted')` - the same predicate as T401's per-subject index and as `OPEN_HANDOFF_STATUSES`, pinned equal to both, and a plain `IN` list because that is the predicate form the fixture world's DDL parser honours. A learner is `enrollment:<id>` to a decision and `lead:<id>` to the reply hook, so T401's per-SUBJECT index could not see both: T414 reproduced the learner decision's admissions handoff and a `READY_TO_ENROLL` reply's admissions handoff open and assigned at once, for one person. `createHandoff`'s replay lookup gains a third `Op.or` arm, `{ lead_id, status IN open }`, when the subject has a lead, so a unique violation from either open index lands on the row that holds it. **8A**: the packet's `escalation_reason` becomes a LIST. `handoffs/escalationTriggers.ts` (new, pure) builds an entry `{ source, queue, reason, decision_id, decision_reason, at }`, reads a Phase 4 packet's single object as that list's first entry so a pre-T501 row still accepts a second trigger, and dedupes on `(source, queue, reason)` - the same trigger replayed is one entry, and the same reason asked of another queue is two. When a trigger lands on an OPEN row, the writer appends it and writes one `growth_journey.handoff.trigger_appended` ledger row; the merged packet is re-checked with `assertPacketCarriesNoAddress` before it is written. The update is guarded on `updated_at` and bounded at `TRIGGER_APPEND_ATTEMPTS` (3): another writer in between makes it match no row, so the row is re-read and re-merged rather than overwritten, and if every attempt loses, the packet keeps what it had and the trigger survives as a `growth_journey.handoff.trigger_append_conflict` ledger row. The commercial-plus-review case the Phase 5 packet named is now visible on the row: a decision that asks for both writes `commercial_state:<state>` AND `human_review_overlay`, where Phase 4 kept only the first.
  - Verification: 15 suites, 268 tests green (`jest -c jest.ci.config.ts` over the handoff, fixture-world and schema suites), including a new `handoffService.phase5.test.ts` (14 tests) whose in-memory store enforces all three unique rules and whose static `update` honours the `updated_at` guard, so the concurrency cases are real lost-update races: another writer between the read and the write keeps BOTH triggers, a row that moves on every attempt stops at 3 and lands in the ledger as a conflict, and a row closed underneath takes no entry. A new `ensureGrowthJourneySchema.phase5.test.ts` pins the index text, its position after Phase 4 and before the ALTER, the shared predicate, the 500-line ceiling and a control-byte tripwire over the new modules. The fixture-world twin is in `handoffRuns.phase4.scenarios.test.ts`: the Training learner's decision opens admissions under `enrollment:<id>`, a `READY_TO_ENROLL` reply for the same lead comes in under `lead:<id>`, one open row survives, and the row names both triggers. Five mutations, each reverted by string replace and md5-verified, each attributed by the tests it broke, over a real 268-test baseline: dropping the per-person arm (9 tests), not recording the trigger (9), deduping on the reason alone (1), narrowing the predicate to `queued` (3), and not shipping the index at all (7). Scoped `tsc` over all 14 files exit 0, with a deliberate `TS2322` reported in 14 of 14 and removed, md5 identical. Pins moved deliberately: the handoff index count 5 -> 6, `mine` 15 -> 16, the fixture world's `UNIQUES` now read from both statement modules, and the Phase 5 module added to the statements tripwire family.
  - Notes: production carries 0 handoff rows, so the index is additive with nothing to backfill; rollback is `DROP INDEX IF EXISTS growth_journey_handoffs_open_lead_unique`. The plan's acceptance 2 said the manual-trigger scenario would end with two entries; it ends with three, because the decision that opens it already asks for two (commercial and review) and the operator's rule is the third - the test asserts all three by name.

The strategies are unchanged: the B2B and learner generators already drop the commercial `create_handoff` while `RETURNED_TO_AI` is on. The packet's disposition text says what `qualified` now does. Time lifts the hold; nothing clears it.

The exit criterion's "whole run, replayed" now expects ONE new decision - sales' verdict is a new input, and the replay WAITs under `RETURNED_TO_AI` with no handoff - then asserts that replaying THAT decision is a no-op and every other count is unchanged. The route test's "qualified -> dispositioned with no cooldown" became "dispositioned AND a 30-day hold"; that file is 550 lines, over the ceiling, so its four assertions were changed in place with no net line added (no split was owed, because no code was added). - [x] T503: the execution schema - receipts, the operator's controls, and in-app nudges
  - Date: 2026-09-20
  - Session: CC-20260812-k4m9
  - What changed: three additive tables in the Phase 5 sibling module, with their models and the fixture world that enforces them. `growth_journey_executions` is the RECEIPT, and it exists because the campaign engine cannot promise exactly once: `scheduled_emails` has no unique index, `campaign_leads`' unique pair lives only in its Sequelize model, and the sequence's own de-dupe is a racy pre-count blind to rows already sent. So the journey carries the guarantee in three database mechanisms - `decision_id` UNIQUE, one OPEN receipt per `(lead, brand, channel)` AND per `(enrolment, brand, channel)` (partial on five open statuses the model exports as `OPEN_EXECUTION_STATUSES`, pinned equal to the index predicate), and the atomic `approved -> enrolling` claim T510 will add. It points at the existing engine's rows (`campaign_id`, `sequence_id`, `scheduled_email_id`, `nudge_id`) deliberately WITHOUT foreign keys - those tables are not this run's to constrain and a receipt must outlive an archived campaign - while hanging off its decision with a real one. `growth_journey_execution_controls` is the operator's switchboard: `rollout` rows raise one brand x programme x channel to `review` or `limited`, `pause` rows lower a brand, programme, channel or single subject to `off`, and the DATABASE refuses an unbounded `limited` (a CHECK demands a cohort and a positive daily limit) and a pause that is not `off`. There is no global scope here on purpose - the global stop stays the existing `system_kill_switch`, so there is one global switch rather than two that can disagree; `brand_id` is nullable only on this table, because a pause may be programme- or channel-wide, and `tenant_id` never is. `growth_journey_in_app_nudges` is the in-app surface, thin by design (title, href, shown/dismissed) with NO body and NO address column: the community bell could not be reused - it is keyed on `community_members`, its types are CHECK-constrained and its digest SENDS EMAIL. Before any of it was added, `models/index.ts` (2,202 lines, far over the ceiling) was SPLIT: the eleven Growth Journey models moved verbatim into `models/growthJourneyModels.ts`, re-exported with one `export *`, so every `import { ... } from '../../models'` is unchanged and the index went DOWN to 2,184 lines.
  - Verification: 7 suites / 155 tests green (the six schema suites plus a new `executionWorld.phase5.test.ts`, 18 tests, in which the fixture world reads its unique indexes from the SHIPPED DDL - so "one receipt per decision", the two one-open slots and "one active control per scope" are the database's rules in the world, not the test's); all of `src/db/__tests__` 57 suites / 649 tests. Five mutations over the real 155-test baseline, each reverted md5-identical and each attributed by the tests it broke: shipping the decision index as a plain index (4 tests, including the pre-existing parity test that every `_unique` name is declared UNIQUE), dropping `in_progress` from the open predicates (2), a model column the DDL never declares (1), a `limited` rollout that need not name a cohort or a limit (1), and two receipt columns swapping places (1 - the columns are pinned IN ORDER, not as a set). Scoped `tsc` over 13 files including `models/index.ts`: exit 0, with a deliberate `TS2322` reported in 13 of 13 and removed md5-identical. Full unfiltered CI: 24,479 passed, 2 failed - the two known Windows CRLF Reese suites, which fail on plain main too; nothing else, which is the proof the model-index split broke no consumer.
  - Notes: two of my own guards fired as false positives before the suite went green - the no-PII column scan flagged `subject_ref` (the journey's subject, not a mail subject) and `scheduled_email_id` (a pointer, not an address); both are allowlisted BY NAME and the allowlist itself is pinned, so a third such column has to be added on purpose. The mutation harness also needed a fix: a needle spanning a line break missed, because this worktree's working copy is CRLF while the statements file was authored LF; it now tries both endings, and the mutation that exposed it was re-run rather than assumed.

- [x] T503 fix: the controls CHECK now refuses what it claimed to refuse, proven against a real PostgreSQL
  - Date: 2026-09-20
  - Session: CC-20260812-k4m9
  - What changed: **a correction to the T503 entry above.** That entry, the commit and the test title all said "the DATABASE refuses an unbounded `limited` rollout"; the shipped CHECK did not. The independent verifier executed the DDL against PostgreSQL 16 and found the hole: a Postgres CHECK is satisfied when its expression is TRUE **or NULL**, so `mode='limited'` with a cohort and a NULL `daily_limit` evaluated to NULL and was ACCEPTED - an unbounded limited rollout, a cohort with no daily cap - as was an EMPTY cohort (`array_length('{}', 1)` is NULL), and as was a `kind='rollout'` row with a NULL `brand_id`, which would raise the mode across every brand in the tenant and contradicts both "one brand x programme x channel" and the stated reason for that column being nullable at all. The test only string-matched the CHECK text, so nothing caught it. Fixed IN PLACE (this DDL has never run anywhere, and `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` would skip an existing table, so an ALTER would have been the wrong instrument): `CHECK (kind <> 'rollout' OR brand_id IS NOT NULL)` is added, and the limited CHECK becomes the NULL-proof `CHECK (mode <> 'limited' OR (COALESCE(daily_limit, 0) > 0 AND COALESCE(array_length(cohort_lead_ids, 1), 0) > 0))`. The schema test now pins the COALESCE form, pins the absence of the NULL-admitting form, and names the three cases in its comment. Acceptance 6's missing half is also built: `models/__tests__/growthJourneyModelsBarrel.test.ts` imports all fourteen Growth Journey models THROUGH `models/index.ts` and checks each is bound to the table its DDL creates - the verifier's own mutation (deleting a re-export) previously left every suite green and the scoped `tsc` at exit 0, and for the three NEW models nothing in the repo imports them from the barrel at all. The stale `phase2.test.ts` title ("fifteen tables" while asserting 18) is corrected.
  - Verification: the fix is proven BY EXECUTION, not by string match - an ephemeral `postgres:16-alpine` (removed afterwards; no production contact) ran the shipped CREATE TABLE and nine cases: the three that were wrongly accepted are now refused, the three that were already refused still are, and the three valid rows (a channel-wide pause with no brand, a bounded limited rollout, a review rollout) still insert. 8 suites / 170 tests green. Four more mutations, each reverted md5-identical and each attributed by name: restoring the NULL-admitting CHECK (1 test), removing the rollout-brand CHECK (1), dropping an EXISTING model's re-export (2), dropping a NEW model's re-export (2). Scoped `tsc` exit 0, and - the gate I had not run, and the only one that caught the dropped re-export before the barrel test existed - the FULL project `tsc -p tsconfig.json --noEmit`, exit 0. Full unfiltered CI: 24,493 passed, 3 failed - the two known Windows CRLF Reese suites, plus `enrollmentRoutes` on the same "Exceeded timeout of 5000 ms" load flake seen in T502's run, which passes isolated (4 tests) here as it did there.
  - Notes: the mutation harness could not revert a mutation whose replacement was an empty string - it then searched for `''` - and one run left the rollout CHECK deleted. I restored it by string replace, re-checked the file, and reshaped every deletion mutation to swap in a comment marker so each can revert; no `git checkout` / `stash` / `restore` was used. The lesson worth carrying: a schema claim that a test can only string-match is not proven until the DDL has been executed somewhere.

- [x] T504: the mode resolver, the scoped pauses, and a kill switch that fails CLOSED
  - Date: 2026-09-20
  - Session: CC-20260812-k4m9
  - What changed: TWO questions, deliberately not one. `resolveExecutionMode` answers "may a NEW execution be planned?" and walks the whole ladder - master flag (`off/flag_master_off`), `journeyExecution` (`shadow/flag_execution_off`, or `observe` when decisions are off too), the channel's Explorer sub-flag (`shadow/explorer_flag_off:<flag>`), `sms`/`voice` (`off/channel_not_authorized`, refused before any row or switch is read), the global kill switch (`off/kill_switch`, or `off/kill_switch_unreadable`), the most specific active pause (`off/pause:<scope>`), the rollout row (`review`/`limited`, none = `shadow/no_rollout`), then cohort membership and the UTC-day limit, with `ali_outreach` capped at `review` whatever the row says. `resolveExecutionHold` answers the narrower "must work ALREADY IN FLIGHT stop?" and reads only the flags, the switch and the pauses: clearing a rollout, removing someone from a cohort or spending the day's quota stops the NEXT plan, it does not cancel an email already enrolled. Both share one `resolveStops`, so planner and hold cannot disagree. Step 3 is an AND across both flag families - either off means off - which is how the legacy Explorer flags and the Growth Journey ones are prevented from disagreeing silently, without a resolver that could itself drift. `scopeKey.ts` is pure and is the one place a scope-less pause is REFUSED: `pause|*|*|*|*` would be a second global switch beside `system_kill_switch`, which section 14 forbids; `pauseScopeKeysFor` returns the fifteen keys that could cover a target, most specific first, so "which pause stopped it" is a fact about the key rather than a judgement in code. `launchSafety.ts` gains `isKillSwitchActiveStrict()` - the same key and the same truth as `isKillSwitchActive`, which is untouched, but it THROWS where that one answers false. The caller treats a throw as ON: this is the only place in the run that fails closed on a read error, because the switch that decides whether a person may be contacted must not read as "off" because a query failed.
  - Verification: 4 suites / 58 tests green, plus the four guards (dark-launch flags, Explorer flags, no-send paths, redaction) at 97. Six mutations over the real 58-test baseline, each reverted md5-identical and each attributed by name: reading the rollout BEFORE the stops (11 tests), matching a subject pause on the brand alone (20), removing the Ali cap (1), letting the hold consult the daily limit (2), an unreadable switch reading as off again (2), and allowing a scope-less pause (1). Scoped `tsc` over 8 files exit 0 with a deliberate `TS2322` reported in 8 of 8 and removed md5-identical. Full unfiltered CI: 24,552 passed, 2 failed - only the two known Windows CRLF Reese suites, which fail on plain main too. SMS and voice are proven blocked on BOTH halves: the resolver refuses them by construction, and `consentGateSmsVoice.test.ts` (12 tests) drives the REAL `consentService` with only its data sources mocked, in `enforce` - the mode production actually runs - showing no record and a non-express basis both blocked, `express_written` / `double_opt_in` allowed, a revoked record blocked, `evaluateSend` naming `consent_no_express_consent`, the control that email is NOT blocked without a record, and the contrast that in `shadow` none of it blocks, which is why the journey re-checks consent itself instead of trusting that setting.
  - Notes: two bugs of my own, caught by these tests before anything else saw them - `pauseScopeKeysFor` emitted duplicate keys when the target had no subject (the wildcard and the named value coincide), and one test asserted an `email` result while seeding only an `in_app` rollout. The key list is now deduplicated and pinned at fifteen keys with a subject and seven without.

- [x] T505: the campaign registry, the campaign validator, the seed that respects an approved campaign, and the setup script
  - Date: 2026-09-20
  - Session: CC-20260812-k4m9
  - What changed: `execution/campaignKeys.ts` is the registry - eleven keys, each with an ownership and a home tenant and brand: the Explorer eight (`journey`, colaberry / colaberry-training, pinned equal to `EXPLORER_CAMPAIGNS` by a test because this file cannot import the seed), the two Layer 2 discovery-question flows (`journey`, one per B2B brand), and `ali_personal_outreach` (`shared` - its hourly cron enrols people with no journey receipt, so a send-time hold that cannot read a receipt must leave those sends as today). It IMPORTS NOTHING, and a test scans it for `import`/`require`: the send path will ask it "is this a journey campaign?" on every campaign-bearing send and must reach "no" for every production campaign with an in-memory lookup. A brand sees only its own keys - AI Flotation never sees a learner key or a Colaberry Business flow. `execution/validateCampaign.ts` refuses, by name: unregistered, missing (found by `settings.campaign_key`, never by name), not brand-scoped (a campaign with no brand would pass the send path's brand gate by default - and all eight Explorer campaigns carry a NULL tenant today, so every one is refused as shipped), not approved (`approval_status` outside `approved|live`), no sequence; and for LIMITED only, campaign not `active` or sequence not `is_active` - a cohort rule releases nothing a human has not switched on. `seedExplorerGrowthCampaigns.ts` now reads the campaign BEFORE it touches the sequence, and an `approved`/`live` campaign's sequence keeps whatever `is_active` the human set; an unapproved one keeps the boot invariant byte for byte, and a new sequence is still created inactive. `scripts/growthJourneyExecutionSetup.ts` is Ali's tool, dry-run by default: `register-campaigns` stamps the home tenant and brand on the eight where NULL (never over a different value - a mismatch is a refusal, and one refusal stops the run) and MERGES `campaign_key` into the existing Ali campaign (found by `CAMPAIGN_NAME` + `CAMPAIGN_TYPE`, now exported from `aliPersonalOutreachService.ts` so the script and the cron cannot find different rows), keeping every other settings key; `create-flow-drafts` creates the two flow campaigns draft, unapproved, test mode, brand-stamped, with a one-step INACTIVE sequence whose instructions ask and never assert - no price, date, start date, deadline, seat count, availability, consent, subscription or compliance claim - and the AI Flotation one never names training. A write needs `--confirm-production` AND `--expect-count N` equal to the plan's write count, so a plan that changed since it was read cannot be applied by muscle memory. The decision table is `execution/setupPlan.ts`, pure and unit-tested; the script only loads and applies, and never activates a campaign, approves one, or touches a sequence's `is_active`.
  - Verification: 8 suites / 100 tests green (five new, plus the three existing Explorer seed suites unchanged), the guards 54. Six mutations over the real 100-test baseline, each reverted md5-identical and attributed by name: skipping the brand compare (4 tests), accepting a draft approval (5), the seed re-asserting `is_active: false` unconditionally (3), the Ali write replacing settings instead of merging (2), the script applying the plan on a dry run (2), and the AI Flotation instructions naming training (1). Scoped `tsc` over 11 files exit 0 with a deliberate `TS2322` reported in 11 of 11 and removed md5-identical. Full unfiltered CI 24,603 passed, 2 failed - only the two known Windows CRLF Reese suites. The eight-plus-one dry-run plan is a test with the create/update spies at zero, and the drafts' creation is asserted to contain no `status: active` and no `is_active: true` anywhere in the payloads.
  - Notes: my own brand-boundary scan fired before the suite went green - the shared guardrail text said "a cohort start", and cohort is learner vocabulary; it now says "a start date", brand-neutral, and the scan stayed as strict as written. Every production run of the script is Ali's; this loop only dry-runs it, and the Phase 6 packet will carry the counts.

- [x] T507: the decision row says whether it may execute
